MINISTRY OF CONSUMER AFE41RS, FOOD AND PUBLIC DlSTRIBUnON 
(Department of Consumer AfEairs) 

New Ddhi, the 22nd Novonba; 2010 

S,0, 560. —Whereas the Central Government, after considering the report submitted to it by the prescribed 
authority, is satisfied that the Model described in the said report (see the figure given below) is in conformity with the 
provisions of the Standards of Weights and Measures Act, 1976 (60 of 1976) and the Standards of Weights and 
Measures (Approval of Modds) Rules, 1987 and the said model is likdy to maintain its accuracy over periods of 
sustained use and to render accurate service under varied conditions; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-sections (7) and (8) of Section 36 of the said Act, the 
Central Government herdty issues and publishes the certificate of approval of the model of non-automatic weighing 
instrument (Weighbridge type) with digital indiedion of medium Accuracy (Accuracy class -HI) of Sdics - WWS-F and 
with brand name " WEL-TCCH" (hereinafter referred to as the said Model), maunfactured by M/s Wel-Tfech Weighing 
Systan, 85, Sarthi Bungalows, Nr RadheTenament, Hari Darshan Chokadi, Kathvada Road, Nava Naroda, Ahmedabad-30 
and which is assigned the ai^rovai mark IND/09/10/206; 

The said modri is a strain gauge ^e load cell based non-automatic weighing instrument (Weighbridge type) 
with digital indication of maximum c^adty of 40 tonne and minimum edacity of 100 kg. The verification scale intoval (e) 
is 5kg. It has a tare device with a 100 percent subtractive retained tare effect. The Light Emitting Diode (LED) indicates 
the weighing results. The instrument opraates on 230 Volts and 50 Hertz alternative current power supply. 


Figure-1 Modd 



Figure-2 Sealing arrangement 


Sealing is done through the holes made in the indicator, then a wire is passed through these holes and the lead seal 
is applied on the wire. A typical schematic diagram of sealing provision of the model is givm above.

MINISTRY OF COAL 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 18th Fdmiary, 2011 

S.O. 580.—Whereas on the publication of the notification of the Govemmait of India in the Ministry of Coal, 
number S.O. 2414 dated the 28th August, 2009 published in the Gaz^te of India, Part - II Section 3, Sub-section (ii), dated 
the 5th September, 2009 issued under Sub- section (1) of Section 9 of die Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and Development) 
Act, 1957 (20 of 1957) (hereinafter referred to as the said Act), the land and all rights in or over the land described in the 
Schedule appended to the said notification (herdnafter referred to as the said land) vested absolutely in the Central 
Government free from all encumbrances under sub-section (1) of Section 10 of the s aid Act; 

And, whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that the Mahanadi Coalfields Limited, IB Block XIII 
(Talabira-II),TalabiraArea, District- Sambalpur., Orissa (hereinafter referred to as the Govemmait Company) is willing to 
comply with such terms and conditions as the Central Government thinks fit to impose in this behalf; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers confeired by sub-section (1) of Section 11 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby direct, that the said land and all rights in or over the said land so vested shall, with effect from the 5th 
September, 2009, instead of continuing to so vest in the Central Government, shall vest in the Government Corqpany, 
subject to the following terms and conditions, namely :- 

1. The Government Company shall reimburse to the Central Govemmeit all payments made in respect of 
compensation, interest, damages and the like, as d^ermined under the provisions of the said Act; 

2. A Tribunal shall be constituted under Section 14 of the said Act, for the purpose of determining the amounts 
payable to the C^tral Government by the Govemmait Company under conditions (1), and all expaiditure incurred in 
connection with any such tribimal and persons ^pointed to assist the tribunal shall be borne by the Government 
Company and similarly, all expenditure incurred in respect of all l^al proceedings like appeals, etc., for or in connection 
with the rights, in or over the said land, so vested, shall also be borne by the Government Company; 

3. The Government Company shall indemnify the Central Government or its officials against any other expenditure 
that may be necessary in connection with any procee^gs by or against the Central Government or its officials, regarding 
the rig)Us in or ova* ihe said land so vested. 

4. The Government Company shall have no power to transfo: the said lands to any other persons without the 
prior approval of the Central Government; and 

5. The Government Company shall abide by such directions and conditions as may be given or imposed by the 
Central Government for particular areas of the said land, as and when necessary. 

[F. No. 43015 / 2 / 2007 -PRrW-I] 
S. C. BHATIA, Director

New Delhi, the 18th Februaiy, 2011 

S.O. 581.—Whereas by the notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of Coal number S.0.1238 
dated the 5th May, 2010, issued under sub-section (1) ofSection 7 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and Development) 
Act, 1957 (2(lof 1957), (hereinafter referred to as the said Act) and published in the Gazette of India, Part - II, Section- 3, 
Sub-section (ii), dated the 15th May, 2010, the Central Government gave notice of its intention to acquire 207.00 hectares 
(approximately) or 511.50 acres (approximately) lands and all rights in or over such land specified in the Schedule appended 
to that notification; 

And, whereas, the competent authority in pursuance of Section 8 of the said Act has made his report to the 
Central Govanment; 

And, whereas, the Central Government after considering the report aforesaid and after consulting the State 
Government of Madhya Pradesh, is satisfied that the lands measuring 207.00 hectares (approximately) or 511.50 acres 
(approximately) and all rights in or over such lands as described in the Schedule appended hereto should be acquired; 

Now, thCTefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of Section 9 of the Coal Bearing Areas 
(Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957, the Central Government hereby declares that llie land measuring 207.00 hectares 
(approximately) or 511.50 acres (ap^MOidmately) and all rights in or ova* such lands as described in the Schedule are hereby 
acquired. 

The plan bearing number NCL/HQ/REVENUE/2010/64 dated the 9th December 2010 of the area covered by this 
notification may be inspected in the office of the Collector Singrauli, Madhya Pradesh or at the offi(« of the Coal 
Controller, I, Council House Street, Kolkata (Pin-700 001) or at the office of the Northern Coalfields Limited, Revenue 
Section, Singrauli, Madhya Pradesh-486889.

S.O. 582.—Whereas by by the notification of the Government of India in the Miaistry of Coal number S.0.1933 
dated the 3rd August, 2010 issued under sub-section (1) of Section 7 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and Development)

Act, 1957 (20 of 1957) (hereinafter referred to as the said Act) and published in the Gazette of India, Part - II, Section 3, Sub¬ 
section (ii) dated the 7th August, 2010, the Central Government gave notice of its intention to acquire 45.996 hectares or 
113.66 acres land as all rights in or over such land specified in the Schedule appended to that notification; 

And whereas the competent authority in pursuance of Section 8 of the said Act has made his report to the Ceiftral 
Govemmait; 

And whereas, the Central Government after considering the aforesaid report and after consultii^ the Government 
of Chhattisgaih, is satisfied that the land measuring 45.996 hectares (approximately) or 113.66 acres (^roximatdy) as all 
rights in or over such lands as described in Schedule appended hereto, should be acquired; 

Now, therefore, in zeroise of the powers conferred sub-section (1) of Section 9 of the sai4 Act, the Cesiral 
Government hereby declares that the knid BwaMwing 45.996 hectares (approximately) or 113.66 acres (^tpioxinxatdy) as all 
rights in or over such land as described in Schedule ^ hereby acquired. 

The Plan bearing number SECL/BSP/GM(PLG)/LAND/ 392, dated the 4th October, 2010 of the area covered by this 
notification may be inspected at the Office of the Collector, Korba (Chhattisgaih) or at the office of the Coal Controller, 1, 
Council House Street, Kolkata-700001 or at the Office of tiie South Easton Coaffield Limited (Revenue Section), Seepat 
Road, Bilaspur-495006 (Chhattisgaih).

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 18th, February, 2011 

S.O. 583.—Whereas on the publication of the notification of the. Government of India in the Ministiy of Coal 
number S.0.2323, dated the 13th Septemba; 2010 published in the Gazette of India, Part - II, Section 3, Sub-section (ii), dated 
the 18 th Sqjtember, 2010 issued under sub-section (1) of Section 9 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acqui sition and Development) 
Act, 1957 (20 of 1957) (hereinafter referred to as the said Act), the lands as All Rights in or over the land described in the 
Schedule appaided to the said notification (hereinafter refored to as the said land) vested absolutely in the Central 
Government free fium all encumbrances under sub-section (1) of Section 10 of the said Act; 

And, whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that the South Eastern Coalfields Limited, Seepat Road, P. B. 
No. 60, District-Bilaspur-495006 (haeinafterreferred to as the Govanment Company) is willing to comply with such terms 
and conditions as the Central Govemmait thinks fit to impose in this behalf; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of Section 11 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby directs that the said land and all rights in or over such land so vested shall, with effect from 
18th Septemba-, 2010, instead of continuing to so vest in the Central Gov«nment shall vest in the Govanment Company, 
subject to the following terms and conditions namely:- 

1. The Government Company shall reimburse to the Central Government all payments made in respect of 
compaisation, interest, damages and the like as determined under the provisions of the said Act; 

2. A Tribunal shall be constituted undo’ Section 14 of the said Act, for the purpose of determining the amounts 
payable to the Central Government by the Government Company under condition (1), and all expenditure incurred in 
connection with any such tribunal and persons appointed to assist the tribunal shall be borne by the Government Company 
and similarly, all expenditure incurred in respect of all legal proceedings like appeals etc. for or in connection with the rights 
in or over the said land so vested shall also be borne by the Government Company; 

3. The Govanment Company shall indemnify the Central Government or its officials against any other expaiditure 
that may be necessary in connection with any proceedings by or against the Central Government or its officials regarding 
the rights in or over the said land so vested; 

4. The Government Company shall have no power to transfer the said land all the rights to any other person 
without the prior approval of the Central Government; and 

5. The Govanment Company shall abide by such direction and conditions as may be given or imposed by the 
Central Government for particular areas of the said land as and when necessary. 


540GI/11—7 


[F. No. 43015/22/2008-PRIW-I] 

S. C. BHATIA, Director

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM AND NATURAL GAS 
New Delhi, the 24th February, 2011 


S. O. 584.—Whereas it appears to Government of India that it i$ necessary in public 
interest that for transportation of natural gas from onshore terrninal at East coast of 
Andhra Pradesh of M/s Reliance Industries Limited to consumers in various parts of the 
country, Kakinada - Basudebpur - Howrah pipeline should be laid by M/s Relogistics 
Infrastructure Limited; 


And whereas, it appears to Government of India that for the purjmse of laying such 
pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the Right of User in land under which the said pipeline 
is proposed to be laid and which are described in the Schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the 
Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in L^) Act, 1962 
(50 of 1962), Government of India hereby declares its intention to acquire the Rigpt of 

User therein; 

Any person interested in the land described in the said Schedule may, vwthin twenty-one 
days from the date on which the copies of the notification as published in the Gazette of 
India under sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the said Act, are m^^e available to the general 
public, object in writing to the acquisition of the Right of U^r therein for laymg Ae 
pipeline under the land to Shri Bhaskar Tripathy, Competent Authority, Relogistics 
Infrastructure Limited, 1st Floor, Fortune Tower, Chandrasekharpur, Bhubanesyvar - 
751023,Orissa State.

AWARD 


The Government of India in the Mumtiy of Labour 
has refored an industrial dispute existing between the em¬ 
ployers in relation to the management of Baisua Iron Mines, 
RMD, SAIL and their workmen in exercise of the powras 
conferred by clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub-section 
2(A) of Section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 vide 
their letterNo. L-2601 l/8/99-IR(M), dated 24/29-12-1999. 

2. The dispute giving rise to the present reference is 
mentioned below. 


"Whether the action of the managment of Barsua 
Iron Mines of RMD, SAIL in not paying canteen 
allowances to the workmen and employees of the 
mines at par with the workmen and employees of 
Meghatabmu, Kiriburu, Bhawanathpur and RMD 
headquarters, Calcutta office employees is justified? 
If not, to what rehef the workmen/employees are en¬ 
titled?" 

3. The 2nd Party-Union repres«iting the workmen 
submitted its statement of claim wherein it has been stated 
that the 2nd Party-Union is a registered trade Union affili¬ 
ated to Bharatiya Mazdoor Sangh and is opaatit^ at Barsua 
Iron Mines and represents the majority of the workmen 
employed in the said mines. The 1st Party-Management is 
a public sector undertaking under the Raw Matmals Divi¬ 
sion, a unit of Steel Authority of India Limited. Initially the 
differait steel plants undCT SAIL namdy Rourkela Steel 
Plant, Bhilai Steel Plant, Durgapur Steel Plant, Bokaro Sted 
Plant etc, had their captive mines to produce and supply 
raw materials required for production of steel in the said 
steel plants. Barsua Iron Mines was one of the captive 
mines of Rourkda Sted Plant at Rourkela till 1990. Later on 
SAIL created a sqparate unit called Raw Materials Division 
and most of the captive mines of different steel plants un- 
da SAIL including the captive mines of RSP were trans¬ 
ferred and brought unda the administrative control of RMD 
with its headquarters at Calcutta, Thus the services of all 
the onployees of Barsua Iron Mines wae placed unda 
Raw Materials Division with effect from 1 -5-1990 without 
any change in their service conditions. Unda the rdevant 
statute the sted plants and the mines are to provide and 
maintain canteens for the use of their employees. Thae- 
fore canteen sd-up was established as pa ^proved speci¬ 
fications and food itons, snacks etc. wae sui^lied on non¬ 
profit basis. Lata, on the plea of cost control the diffdent 
units of SAIL including mines started withdrawing the fa¬ 
cilities provided in the departmental canteens unilaterally. 
The numba of food items and snacks wae reduced and 



1824 


THE GAZETTC OF INDIA:FEBRUARY 26,2011/PHALGUNA 7,1932 


[Past n—SBc.3(ii)] 


the standard was deteriorated. The employees had'no 
choice for food items because of very limit^ items sup¬ 
plied in the canteen and their poor quality. Resultantly ^e 
Unions in different units including the 2nd Party-Union 
started demanding canteen allowance in lieu of canteen 
facilities. Although different units of SAIL including the 
mines under Raw Materials Division and its headquarters 
at Calcutta granted canteen allowance to their employees, 
but the 1 St Party-Management did not pay any heed to the 
rightful demand of its employees. Because of callous 
attitude of the 1st Party-Managenent, the 2nd Party-Union 
had no otho* alternative but to raise an industrial dispute. 
The conciliation officer tried his best for an amicable 
settlement, but the conciliation proceedings ended in fail¬ 
ure and the matter under dispute was referred for adjudica¬ 
tion. The different mines of Raw Materials Division and its 
headquarters at Calcutta are paying canteen allowances as 
under. 

(a) RMD Head Quarters ... Rs. 12.00 } 

(b) Magbataburu ... Rs. 15.50} Per day 

(c) Kiriburu ... Rs. 15.50}actual 

(d) Bhawanatlpur ... Rs. 15.50}atten- 

dance 

4. The denial of canteen allowance to the 2nd Party- 
woilanen at par with the employees of other mines and 
RMD headquarters is hi^y illegal, arbitrary and discriini- 
natory. During pendency of this reference the 1 st Party- 
Management most clandestinely and hurriedly signed the 
settlement behind the back of the 2nd Party-Union for 
payment of Us. 6 per day of actual working days as 
canteen allowance which is lower than the amount paid to 
the similarly situated woika^ in other mines or mines of 
the RMD headquarters. It is interesting to note tibat the 
employees of RMD headquarters at Calcutta, who have no 
contribution for production in mines and only perform 
coordinating job and office work are paid canteen allow¬ 
ance at Rs. 12 per day. The Raw Materials Division being 
one of the units of SAIL with different mines under its 
control denying the workers of Barsua hon Mines the ben¬ 
efits of canteen allowance at par with odier mines and RMD 
headquarters at Calcutta amounts to discriminaticm and 
anti labour practice. Therefore the 2nd Party-workmen are 
entitled to canteen allowance at par with the above mines 
and RMD headquarters at Calcutta from the date the em¬ 
ployees of the said mines have been granted the said 
boiefits. 

5. The 1st Party-Management filed reply through 
its written statement stating that the captive mines of 
Meghatuburu, Kiriburu, Bhawanathpur then under the ad¬ 
ministrative control of Bokaro Steel Plant, Barsua Iron 
Mines, Kalta Iron Mines, Pumapani Limestone and Dolo¬ 
mite Quarry under the administrative control of Rourkela 
Steel Plant and Bolani Ores Mines under the administra¬ 
tive control of Durgapur Steel Plant were kept undo- the 
adminis trative control of Raw Materials Division. Against 
the decision to place the administrative control of the mines 
under Raw Materials Division, the operating unions of 


Rourkela Sted Plant namely Rourkela Shramik Sangh and 
Rourkela MazdoorSabha^ed writ petitions in the High 
Court of Orissa which were duroissed. During the pen¬ 
dency of die writ petition die Ist Party-Management was 
under compulsion to follow the policy/rules of Rourkela 
Steel Plant to avoid any contenqit of court Undkr die Mines 
Act, 1952 the Management is statutorily required to run 
canteen to provide tea, snacks and food items on subsi¬ 
dized rate to the workmen of the establishment. With the 
mnnipg of canteen, no canteen allowance was being paid 
to the employees. The 2nd Party-Union has no mass 
following whereas RomkelaMazdoorSaUia, anotho'rec- 
ognized Union of Barsua Iron Mine is having sizeable 
number of followers in the min es. Bodi the aforesaid Unions 
donanded canteen allowances for the workmen mgaged 
in the establishment of the 1st Party-Management. After 
having several discussions with the recognized Union 
canteen allowance at die rate of Rs. 6 per day was settled 
to be paid to the enqiloyees in heu oflhe canteen facilities 
with ^ect fixim 1-4-2000 and the employees have received 
payments on this score. The Rourkda Sted Plant had in 
fact introduced canteen allowance at the rate of Rs. 6 per 
day with effect from 1-4-1999. The same was taken as basis 
of setdement for Barsua Iron Mines, Kilibuni Iron Mines 
and Purunapani Lime &Dolomite Quarry mines. The 
erstwhile captive mines of die Steel plants were enjoying 
die bai^ts attached to their respective steel plants. The 
conditions of service of theemploy ees woddng dierem are 
different from min e to mine bas^ on the plant to which 
they are attached as cjqitive mines. Settlement widi the 
recognized Unitm on the issue cannot be said to be high 
handed attitude of the 1st Party-Management and anti 
labour practice. The Management of Barsua Iron Mines 
has no say over the j^ahs of RIOM, MION^ Bhawanadipur, 
and Head Quarters of RMD at Catciftta. The reference is 
thus not maintainable under the Industrial Disputes Act as 
th^e is no demand and reftisal to constitute a dispute. 

6. In its rejoinder the 2nd Party-Union has 
r.haHeng ed the authority of the Rourkela Mazdoor Sabha 
to enter into settlement on die ground diat it was granted 
recognition by the Rouikda Steel Plant about 20 years 
back. Recognition status under the^ Code of Disciplioe is 
granted to the Union having majority suRKJrt for a period 
of two years aftor which fresh verification of monbership 
is done to ascertain die majority suppmt and fresh recog¬ 
nition is granted. Therefore, verification of membership 
under the secret ballot has to be done after every two 
years as pw instructions fiom the Government of India. 
H«ice recognition granted to Rouikda Mazdoor Sabha 
aftw two years is not valid. The 1st Party-Man^ement 
has not taken any steps for verification of membership of 
the Union. The said Union is neither a recognized Union in 
the eye of law nor it has m^ority support. The employees 
of differ^t mines hold transferable post. As such if an 
employee is transferred in Kiriburu, Meghataburu and 
Bhawanathpur mines which are paying canteen allowance 
in the name of canteen subsidy at foe rate of Rs. 15.50 pa

day as per actual atteaidance will be financially benefited 
and if the employee from the aforesaid mines is transfored 
to Barsua Iron Mines he will suffer financial loss. 

ISSUES 

7. Following issues wo-e settled on the pleadings of the 
parties for adjudication. 

1. Wh^er the reference is maintainable? 

2. Whether the action of the Management in not 
paying canteen allowances to the workmen and 
employees of the mines at par with the woikmen and 
employees of Meghataburu, Kiriburu, Bhawanathpur 
and RMD HeadquartCTS, Calcutta office employees 
is justified? 

3. If not, to what relief the workmen/employees are 
entitled? 

8. From the side of the 2nd Party-Union one Pravakar 
Mallick has been examined as W.W.I and documents 
exhibited as 1 to 10 have been filed and proved. 

9. The 1st Party-Management has filed Sworn 
Affidavit of Shri R. K. Das, Senior Manager (Vigilance) in 
evidence and filed six number of documents proved and 
exhibited as Ext. A to B/03. 

FINDINGS 

Issue No. 1 

10. The 1st Party-Management has challenged the 
maintainability of the reference in its writtai statement on 
the grounds firstly that no constituents of demand and 
refusal exist here in this case to constitute a dispute undo- 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 and secondly, that the 
1 st Party-Management is not concerned with the affairs of 
KJOM, MIOM, Bhawanathpur and RMD Head Quarters at 
Calcutta. So far as the first ground is concerned it holds no 
good as the 1st Party-Management has itself admitted in 
Para- 7 of its writtwi statement that the 2nd Party-Union 
and Rouricela Mazdoof Sabha, the recognized Union 
demanded canteai allowance in respect of woritmtm 
engaged in the establishment of the 1 st Party-Management. 
Tlierefore it cannot be said that there was no demand for 
canteen allowance from the side of the 2nd Party-Union 
and subsequent refusal by the 1st Party-Management to 
grant canteen allowance which ultimately gave rise to 
conciliation and that referral of dispute to this Tribunal. 
The second ground is also not valid one as the 2nd Party- 
Union is simply demanding canteen allowance at par with 
KJOM, MIOM, Bhawanathpur and RMD Head (garters at 
Calcutta which are similarly situated. In this view of the 
matter it is held that the referoice is maintainable before 
this Tribunal. This issue is decided against the 1st Party- 
Management. 

Issue No. II 

Undisputedly before the year 1990 different units of 
SAIL i.e. Steel Plants had separate captive mines under 
their administrative control. In the year 1990 captive mines 


wore taken back fix»m the administrative control of the 
Steel Plants aiul placed under the Raw Materials Division. 
The captive mines at Meghataburu, Kiriburu, and 
Bhawanathpur under the administrative control of Bokaro 
Steel Plant, Barsua Iron Mines, Kalta Iron Mines, 
Purunapani Lime Stone & Dolomite Quarry under the 
administrative control of Rourkda Steel Plant and Bolani 
Iron Mines undo* the administrative control of Dui^apur 
Steel Plant were kept under the Administrative control of 
Raw Materials Division. All the mines were having 
independent establishments. The erstwhile captive mines 
of the plants are so far mjoying the benefits attached to 
their respective Steel Plants. The Rouricda Steel Plant had 
in fact introduced canteen allowance at the rate pfRs. 6.00 
per day with effect ft'om 1 -4-1999. The same was taken as 
basis of settlemait for Barsua Iron Mines, Kiriburu Iron 
Mines and Punmapani Lime Stone and Dolomite Quarry 
Mines. After negotiation with the recognized Union the 
canteen allowance was introduced in the establishment of 
the 1 st Paity-Management with effect finm 1-4-2000in lieu 
of subsidized facilities. The 1st Par^-Manag^ent has no 
say ovCT the affairs KJOM, MIOM, Bhawanathpur and 
RMD Heiid Quarters at Calcutta. 

12. The 2nd Party-Union has claimed canteen 
allowance at par with the workmaa of other mines i.e. 
Meghataburu, Kiriburu, Bhawanathpur and RMD 
heaclquarters at Calcutta from the date the employees of 
the said mines have been granted the same benefit. The 
2nd Party-Union has quoted the rates of canteen allowance 
at different mines and offices in Paragraph-6 of their 
Statement of claim which are given below:— 

(a) RMD Head Quarters ... Rs.12.00 

(b) Maghataburu ... Rs. 15.50 Per day 

(c) Kiriburu ... Rs.l5.50actual 

(d) Bhawanathpur ... Rs. 15.50 attendance 

13. W.W. 1, Pravakar Mallick deposed to the same 
effect in his statement before the Tribunal. He also stated 
that a.sumofRs. l().60perdayis being paid to the workers 
of Bolani Iron Mines. According to the 2nd Party-Union 
the different rates of canteen allowance being paid to 
different captive mines is.not only illegal and arbitrary but 
also discriminatory. The contention of the 1st Party- 
Management is that the conditions of service of employees 
working in difftrent captive mines are diffoent from mine 
to mine based on the plant to which they are attached. 
Therefore this difference carmot be said to be arbitrary or 
discriminatory. But here it has to be seen as to pwity with 
which rate of canteen allowance is being claimed by the 
2nd Party- Union when rates of canteen allowance at RMD 
Headquarters at Calcutta is said to be Rs. 12.00 per day of 
actual attendance whereas at Meghataburu, Kiriburu, 
Bhawanathpur the rates are Rs. 15.50 per day of actual 
attmdanceand at Bolani Ore Mines it is Rs. 10.60 po*day. 
The 1st Party-Management has led the evidence of 
Shri R. K. Das who has stated that in Rouikda Steel Plant 
canteen allowance has been raised from Rs. 6.00 to 





]826 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: FEBRUARY 26 , 2011/PHALGUNA 7,1932 


[Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 


Rs. 15.00 with effect from 1-4-2005. The same is applicable 
to Barsua Iron Mines along with other mines under the 
administrative control of RMD including RMD, Rourkela 
and Liason Office, RMD, Bhubaneswar. Ext. B/2 is the 
circular (order) oftihe Senior Manager (Personnel) of SAIL, 
RMD, Kolkatta dated 27-4-2005 with regard to the 
enhancement of canteen expenses from Rs. 6.00 to Rs. 15.00 
with effect from 1-4-2005. Therefore only a difference of 
0.50 paise remains to be achieved as canteen allowance by 
the 2nd Party-Union which is being paid in excess to the 
employees of captive mines of Me^ataburu, Kiriburu and 
Bhawanathpur. Since canteen allowance at RMD 
Headquarters is being paid at the rate of Rs. 12.00 per day 
of actual attendance and parity with different rate of canteen 
allowances has been claimed by the 2nd Party-Union, it is 
not clear whether parity at the rate of RMD Headquarters 
or parity at the rate of Meghataburu, Kiriburu, 
Bhawanathpur Mines which are paying Rs. 15.50 per day 
of actual attendance Is to be granted to the 2nd Party- 
Union? The 1st Party-Management has raised this issue 
by saying that when there is no parity in payment of 
canteen allowance in the mines and RMD Headquarters 
with whom the 2nd Party-worionen claim parity the claim of 
the 2nd Party-Union cannot be allowed being Untenable. 
Hence, there is no specified demand in the eye of law. The 
contention holds good. How can parity be granted when 
there is no parity in payment of canteen allowance in various 
captive mines? Thus the demand of parity itself becomes 
fallacious and redundant. Therefore the action of the 1st 
Party-Management in not paying canteen allowance to the 
2nd Party-workmen and employees of the mines at par with 
the workmen and employees of Meghataburu, Kiriburu, 
Bhawanathpur Mines and RMD headquarters, Calcutta 
office employees cannot be described as illegal, unjustified, 
arbitrary and discriminatory. This issue is 
accordingly decided against the 2nd Party-Union. 

issue No. Ill 

14. In view of the findings given under Issue No. 11 
the 2nd Party-woikmen are not entitled to the relief claimed. 

15. Reference is answered accordingly.

of sub-section (1) and sub-section 2 (A) of Section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 have referred the 
following industrial dispute to this Tribunal for 
adjudication: 

“Whether the action ofthe management of Mumbai 
Port Trust in recovering compensation towards 
non-vacation of quarters in respect of 7 employees 
namely S/Sh. D. Y. Sarode, Tindel, K. M. Pojari,

1827 


2. Claim Statement is filed by the second party 
through its Joint Secretary at Ex.-7 stating and contotding 
that, the Management first party unlawfully and 
unauthohsedly recovered from the wages, arrears payment 
and settlement dues in respect of these concerned sevai 
woricmen the following amount on the plea of recovery of 
compensation for alleged unauthorised use and 
occupation or erstwhile BDLB quarters by these woricmen 
viz D.Y. Sarode Rs. 30,849.80, K.M.Pujari Rs.20,942.87, 

R. S. Jaiswal Rs. 71,326.96, Sitaram MahiigiRs. 26,693.03, 

S. M.Jadhav Rs. 39,163.78, Shaikh Usman Rs. 1,49,928.48 
and Krishna T. Salian Rs.45,201.54. It is case of the second 
party Union that the action of the management in 
recovCTing the above amounts from the wages, arrears 
payments and from the settlement dues in respect of the 
above named workmen is illegal and unjustifi^ and is in 
violation of principles of natural justice, in contravention 
of the provisions made under the Industrial Disputes Act, 

1947 as well as Payment of Wages Act, 1936. It is case of 
the second party union that, it amounts to unfair labour 
practice on the part of the Management. According to 
them, the said workmen are entitled to receive the said 
amounts with 18% interest and officers of the managemmt 
responsible for such unauthorized and unlawful 
deductions are liable for severe punishm^ts. It is therefore, 
prayed by the second party Union to hold the action of 
the first party in recovering the above amounts as illegal 
and unjustified and the concOTied woikmen are aititled 
to receive the said amounts with 18% interest thereon and 
the officers responsible for such recoveries are liable to 
be penalized heavily. 

3. First party resisted the statement of claim vide 
their written statement Ex-8 stating and contending that 
the concerned workmen were allottees of residential 
quarters at Ex-Bombay Dock Labour Board Colonies. They 
sub-leted their quarters to other employees/outsiders and 
thus violated the provision of Regulation 21 of the MBPT 
Employees’ (Allotment and Occupancy of Residential 
Regulation) 1975. It is case of the first party that said 
workmen were only licensee and not tenants and hence 
they were liable to vacate the respective quartos afro the 
order of cancellation. It is case of the first party, before 
ordering them to vacate the quarters, notices of 
cancellation of allotment of quarters were issued to each 
of them as required under the regulations indicating that 
on failure to deliver up the vacant possession of the 
quarters allotted to them, they shall have to pay 
compensation for use and occupation of quarters from the 
date on which the cancellation became effective till the 
date of actual vacation at the rate fixed for the same. It is 
case of the first party that inspite of the receipt of 
cancellation notices, the employees continued to stay in 
the quarters unauthorisedly. It is case of the first party 
that these employees overstayed in the quarters. Therefore, 
recovery of compensation for use and occupation of the 
residences and other charges were deducted from their 
salary, as direct recovery of compensation for unauthorized 
use and occupation of quarters and other charges as 


permissible in terms of notices of cancellation, as 
permissible under clause (D) of sub-clause (2) of Sectmn 7 
of the Payment of Wages Act, 1936. It is case of the first 
party that, the Bombay Dock Labour Board had merged 
with the Mumbai Port TVust on 1-3-1994 and on merger, 
the registered workers of the Ex. BDLB were absorbed 
with the Mumbai Port Trust and that upon absorption as 
a sqiarate ^tity the BDLB en^iloyees/r^istered workers 
would acc^t all the conditions of enqiloyment as are 
presently jqiplicable to the en^loyees of the Mumbai Port 
Trust in all respects including ovor time and house rent 
allowances, recovery of rent etc. and they will be subject 
to all rules, bye-laws, r^ulations of service conditions 
applicable to the regular ^i^loyees of Mumbai Port Trust 
in terms of clause (i) and (xviii) of the Memorandum of 
settlement dated 25-1-1994 and clauses (vii) and (viii) of 
the Memorandum of Agre^ent dated 13-12-1991. 

4. It is case of the first party that Ex-BDLB 
employees on absorption are govaued by the same set of 
rules and regulations of the Mumbai Port Trust and in 
1997, this Tribunal by its judgem^t dated 28-11-1997 
dismissed some applications under Section 33 C (2) of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, filed by Ex-BDLB en^oyees 
in application Nos.LC-2/8,9,10 and 11 of 1995 for getting 
over time for working beyond 6 & 1/2 hours per day and 
for monetary benefits for working on Satiudays and 
Sundays which are paid holidays as not maintainable 
upholding the cont^tions of the Port Administration that 
the employees of the erstwhile Bombay Dock Labour 
Board are to accept all the conditions of enqiloyment as 
are fqiplicable to the en^loyees of MBPT in all respects in 
accordance with the four clauses mentioned herein. It is 
case of the first party that the imauthorized occupation of 
quartos afl^ cancellations of the same and refusal to pay 
conqiensation for use and occupation of quarters and ofrer 
charges, therefore, is essoitially is a misconduct on the 
part of the employees. It is contended by the first party 
that the matter of recovery of compensation for 
unauthorized ovastay by the employees was not referred 
to the Estate Officer under Section 7 of the Public Premises 
(Eviction of unauthorized occupations) Act, 1971. It is 
further contended by the first party that direct recovery of 
compensation for unauthorized use and occupation of 
quarters and other charges is pamissible in terms of 
Regulation 21 of the MBPT Employees (Allotment & 
Occupancy of Residential) Regulation, 1975. It is therefore, 
contended by the first party that it has recovo’ed the 
compensation fit)m employees concerned in the above 
reference afto^ following all the rules and regulations 
applicable to the MBPT Employees and is therefore, legal 
and justified and such deductions are also permissible 
under clause (d) of sub-section (2) of Section 7 of the 
Paymmt of Wages Act, 1936 and its action is legal and 
justified and pray that the refer^ce be rejected. 

5. Rejoinder is filed by the Joint Secretary of the 
second party Union at Ex.-9 rqieating the same story as 
made in Claim Statonoit and denying the contentions 


1828 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: FEBRUARY 26,2011/PHALGUNA 7,1932 


[Part II— Sec*. 3(ii)] 


raised by the first party in their Writtai Statement which 
are contrary to the statement of claim. 

6. In view of the above pleadings, following issues 
were framed by my Learned Predecessors at Ex-10, which 
I answa as under: 

Issues 

1. Whether the action of the management of Mumbai 
Port Trust in recovering compensation towards 
non-vacation of quarters in respect of 7 employees 
namely S/Sh. D.Y. Sarode, K. M. Piyari, R. S. Jaiswal 
Sitaram Mahangi, M. Jadhav, Shaikh Usman and 
Krishna T. Salian is legal and justified? 

2. What relief the workm^ are elititled? 

Reasons 

Issue No. 1: 

7. In the case at hand, the seven workmen were 
admitedly in the service of Mumbai Port Trust. They wo'e 
allotted residmtial quarters. During the courseofsurprise 
inspection, it was found that these workmen have sub¬ 
letted their respective quarters to some others. Therefore, 
the managemoit of Mumbai Port Trust have cancelled the 
allotmoit and asked the respective workmen to pay the 
charges for occupying the quarters even after cancellation 
of their respective allotments. It is a fact that, as these 
workCTs have not deposited the charges of the quarters, 
the management deducted the charges from their wages 
and arrears of payment. According to the workmen, the 
said recovery of compensation for alleged unauthorized 
use and occupation of BDLB quarters was illegal action 
on the part of the managmott. According to diem, neither 
they were served with show cause notice nor any 
opportunity of hearing was given to them. It amount to 
violation of principles of natural justice. In this respect, 
the learned ^vocate for the management has submitted 
with list Ex-20 page Nos. I to 16 the copies of the notices 
with signature/thumb impression of the respective 
workman for having recdved the show cause notice. From 
these office copies of the notices it is clear that show 
cause notices were given to the respective workmen. After 
hearing the workmen, the concmied officer cancelled the 
allotmoit of respective workers. ThCTeafter the order of 
cancellation was also served on each of the worka and 
they were directed to pay the compensation for occupying 
the quarters unauthoris^y after the ord^ of cancellation. 
The cancellation ordas were served on the workmen. 
OfiBcecopiesthereofarewithlistEx-19 pagelto 13.Some 
of the employees have made application to the President . 
of Mumbai Port Trust and requested to cancel the orders 
of cancellation of residential quarters. Their 
representation/appeal to Chairman/President, Mumbai 
Port Trust copies thereof is on record with Ex-19 of page 

15 to 18. The Chairman has dismissed their appeals. 

8. From the facts and circumstances on record, it is 
revealed that the residential quarters allotted to the 
respective workers, were found occupied by some others 


therefore, these seven workmen were served with show 
cause notices. After hearing them, the order was passed 
and their respective allotmmts were cancdled. Four of 
the woikers have pneferred appeals. The copies of their 
appeals are at Ex-19 page 15 to 18. Their ^^peals were also 
dismissed. In this background, the plea of the woricmen 
does not stand to the reasons that ^ey w^e not served 
with notice and no opportunity was given to them of being 
heard. There was no violation of principles of naturd 
justice. The inquiry was just and proper. 

9. For the facts and circumstances on record, it is 
also revealed that these workers haveretained possession 
of their quartos even aftw cmicellation of their respective 
allotment. Therefore, they were liable to pay the 
conqioisation and water charges, etc. to the Mumbai Port 
Trust. In the circumstances, 1 hold that the action of 
mwagement of Mumbai Port Trust is just and proper. 
Therefore, it needs no interference. Accordingly, I decide 
issue no. 1 in the affirmative. Consequently 1 hold that the 
workmoi are not entitled to any rdief. Accordingly I decide 
issue no. 2 in the negative and proceed to pass the 
following ordo': 

The action of the management of Mumbai Port Trust 

in recovering the conq>ensation from their seven 

employees referred above is just, propw and legal.

4. On the basis of pleadings of the parties following 
points emerge in this case for decision 

(i) Whether termination of services of the 32 
workmen is justified ? 

(ii) Whether non-payment of legal dues is 
justified ? 

(iii) To what relief the workmen are entitled to ? 

5. At the very outset it was argued on behalf of the 
nuinagement that dereference itself is bad. There was no

mind. There was no material before the Government to

or the union before management and refusal ofthe said 
demand by the management. I do not find’ any merit in 
1 h is argument. Once a referaice is made this tribunal has 
no jurisdiction to examine if the reference is bad or 
good. This tribunal can not go behind the refa-ence. The 
tribunal is obliged to answer the reference. Moreover, this 
IS a case of termination of service of 32 workmen by the

By OrdaNo.L-32011/6^001-IR(M)dated 10-08-2001 
the Govemmqit of India. Ministry of labour in acerciseof

ofpay of Rs.2250—4430/- to Sh. Basudeb Bera, Anil

to?" 

2. One application has beai filed today from the 
side of the workmen expressing that the dispute has been 
resolved in between the parties which is a result of mutual 
understanding in between the parties and the workman 
does not want to proceed with the present reference in 
view of thesaid mutual understanding. Representative from 
the management ride also agreed and endorsed such pniyer 
of the workman concerned who is posonally present today. 
Since Mr. Bhadury the authorize represoitative of the 
workmen union is present, I think endorsement of the 
workmen union itself in the application is not needed as he 
stated that the workmen union has knowledge of the said 
mutual understanding. 

In view of the said jM^ayer in the application filed 
today and as per oral version made by the workman on a 
query'from my side, I am satisfied that the prayer made on 
behalf of the workmoi is genuine. Let the present reference 
be disposed of as resolved in between the parties outside 
the Tribunal. An Award is passed accordingty. 

JUSTICE MANIKMOHAN SARKAR, Presiding Officer

In exercise of the powers confared under Clause (d)

“Whether the action of the management of Genaal

is justified and propa? If not, to what relief the above 
workman entitled?” 

Notices wae issued to both parties and thaeafta

3. In nut shell it is the case of the workman that he 
was employed as a tank truck helpa with Party II at its

Coiporation Ltd. since 1980. He was served with a charge

Regional Office. The enquiry was instituted against the 
workman by the Genaal Managa (SOD) of H.P.C.L. Head 
Quarta at Mumbai. Aftathe said enquiry the management 
took a daision to terminate the services of the workman 
vide lata dated 9-11-1990 issued by Chief General Managa

against proposed penalty. Howeva a copy of the findings 
and the rq^ort of the enquiry offica was not givoi which

Vide letta dated 21-11-90 workman replied to the show

at Madras issued a letta dated 20-3-91 thaeby imposing 
pumshment of termination of services with immediate effect. 
The workman claimed that the aiquiiy was initiated by the 
Head Quarter at Bombay and haice the Gaeral Managa, 
the South Zone, Madras had no jurisdiction to issue the 
tamination orders. The workman approached appellate 
authority. Howeva, the chairman and the Managing 
Dirator failed to consida all these aspats and rejated 
the appeal. The workman thaeafta raised the dispute and 
since the conciliation failed, the matta was referred to this 
Tribunal. 

4. The employa in their written statement claimed 
that the workman committed serious misconduct by 
ammitting thdl of the company property and therefore 
the charge sheet was issued to him and enquiry was 
conducted in fair and proper matma by giving full 
opportunity. The workman in clear terms admitted his guilt 
along with the otha employee regarding aspect of theft 
and apart from that management proved alleged 
misanduct against the workman by indq^endent evidence. 
Thaeafta show cause notice was issued to the workman 
regarding proposed punishmat and since Vasco office is 
coming within the jurisdiction of South Zone, the orders 
of termination was issued by the ampetent authority. In 
the rejoinda filed at Ext.7 the workman daied all aspects

in the written statement and claimed that there is no 
material to prove the misconduct and hence order of

5. After considering rival contention issues were 
framed vide Ext, 8 and additional issue was framed vide 
Ext. 13. Issue no. 1 and lA were treated as preliminary issues 
and thereafter both parties led evidaice. An order was 
passed vide Ext.21 dated 4-12-2008 by my learned 
predecessor thereby deciding both preliminary issues. In 
the said order it was observed that issue No. 1 is answered 
in negative whereas issue No. 1A is answered in affirmative. 
Thus remaining three issues are with respect to the 
punishment aspect and the same are considered at present. 
The Party I steppe^nto the witness box vide Ext.22 and 
claimed that die punishment of termination of services is 
shockingly dispropotationate and his past record was 
clean and unblemished, TTie Party II did not lead any 
evidence with regard to the present issues. Both parties 
submitted written iu^ments which are at Ext. 23 and 24 
respectively, 

6 . I have perused the said written submisions as 
well as the aitire record. Issue reads as undCT together 
with my findings against it 

1 Whether Party I proves 
that the action of the 
Party II in terminating 
the services of Party 
I is vitiated for not 
supplying the copy of 
the fmdings and report 

of the enquiry officer ? Negative (vide order

7. Issue No.2: In the preswit matter this Tribunal 
vide ordo* dated 4-12-2008 has already helddiatUieaiquiry 
was held in fair and proper manner and the misconduct are 
proved to the satisfaction of the Tribunal by acceptable 
evidene. The misconduct alleged against the workman is 
found in the charge sheet which is a part and parcel of the 
enquiry proceedings at Ext.E-5 colly. The diarge sheet 
dated 29-8-88 show that there are four charges levelled 
gainst workman (1) theft or dishonesty in connection 
with the EnqiloyCT’s business or property; (2) Wilful 
damage to or loss of Employer’s goods or property; (3) 
Taking bribes; (4) Wilful insubordination in connection 
with Employer’s property. The fact leading to the above 
charges are that on March 1988 the workman was on 
duty in first shift at Vasco Terminal and he was assigned 
the duty to accompany Shri J. M. Palha, H. V. Driver to 
carry 8 KL of M. S. (Petrol) md 4 KL of HSD (Diesel) in 
tanker truck No. GDU 4650 forthe supply to retail outiet of 
M/s K.W,, Kenny and Associates Panjim as pa- invoice 
No. 005409. The seal of the taidc treuk was ch^ed by the 
Operation Officers befoere the treuk left the terminal. At 
around 10.00 a.m. on 3-3-88 Deputy Manager and Bulk 
operator went to the retail outlet to check the contents of 
the tanker truck No. GDU 4650 and they found that seals 
of the compartment no.l & 2 were tendered and the 
samples takm out from the tanker trude in presence of the 
workman aid the driver failed in the filter piq)a- test. 

8 . During the investigation the workman and the 

driver admitted in writing that they removed one barrel 
(approximately 200 litres) of the product from each 
compartment and added equal quantities of Kerosene after 
tampering with die seals. They fiirther admitted that they 
collected an amount of Rs. 1200 towards sale of removed 
product out of which Rs. 500 was kept by die workman 
and the r emainin g by the driver. The chaise sheet was 
signed by the Senior Regional Manager Belgaum Regional 
Office and thereafter enquiry was conducted against the 
workman wherdn he participated and was afforded fair 
oi^>ortuiiitytodef(OTdhiBas^,; , / - . 

9. In the presait mall^ the 

tamination order isriied by Managii, l§oudi Zone 

^as no authority but the workman failed to pcoduce any 
mat^al to that effect. The only evidei^ by the 
Wpi’kmari is regarding jpunishment #hich is 
disproportionate to the misetmduct ^d nothing else. 
However, writtm submission filed by Party II cleariy show 
that the Vasco tominal comes wiffiin the jurisdiction of 
South Zone and therefore die General Manager is the 
competaitaulhortyto issue ffie order of terminati<Hi. Since 
the workman has failed to disclose any material to prove 
his contention regarding issue No. 2 and also tiiere is no 
reference or arguments advanced in the written 
submissions at Ext. 23, it is to be considered that the 
woikman failed to discharge the bm-den regardii^ issue 
No. 2 and hoice the same is answered as not proved. 





[^II—3(ii)3 


: 'qR5R^26,2011/WpT7, 1932 


1837 


10. Issue Nos. 3 & 4: It is the cont^tion of the 
workman that the termination order dated 20-3-1991 is 
illegal, imiMoper and m^usttfied. In this respect the evidence 
of workman at Exb. 22 discloses that his past record was 
imblemished and therefore he could have been awarded 
lesser punishment instead of dismissing from service. He 
further claimed that after the dismissal, he was unen^loyed 
for a period of two years and now he is working at STD 
booth with a salary of Rs. 2000 per mondi. The written 
submission filed at Exb. 23 on behalf of the workman shows 
that the misconduct alleged against the workman was not 
serious warranting maximum punishment and it was 
involving meager amount. It is also claimed that there was 
no loss suffered by Party II. Further the workman was not 
suspended during the ^quiry till the ord^" of dismissal 
and therefore it showed that the employo* did not lose 
confidence with Party I and hence the punishment is 
disproportionate, harsh and unwarranted. 

11. The written submission filed on bdialf of Party 
II, refa^ to various decisions with regard to the imposition 
of punishmmt and on that basis it has been claimed that 
when the employee is found guilty for the misconduct of 
a grave nature like theft of the propaty of the employa, 
punishment of dismissal cannot be considered as 
disproportionate. 

12. While deciding preliminary issues, this Tribunal 
has confirmed the contentions of Party 11 with regard to 
the charges levelled against the workman and acceepted 
the material placed before it to hold that the workman was 
found guilty of all the charges levelled against him. 
Considerig this aspect, it is but natural to lose faith and 
confidence with such employee by the employer as the 
property was entrusted to be delivered safely and the same 
was tampered with during the transit. Apart from it the 
workman was involved in adulteration of the product 
which was in the tank by adding Kersone to it in order to 
bring bulk at the original level and thereaft er by selling the 
removed product to third party and appropriating the 
amount clandestinely. The behaviour of the workman 
certainly bring disrepute to the good will of the company 
and also to retail or as contaminated product is supplied 
with. Such case amount to committing theft, irrespective 
of the account invloved in it and such acts certainly cause 
a huge loss and disrepute of the employer in all respects. 
The seals of the tanker truck were also tampered with for 
the purpose of removing the product shored in it and thus 
It also amounts in damaging the property of the employer 
ibr uUerrior motive. 

13. In the case of Depot Manager APSRPC V/s 
Raghuda Siva Sankar Prasad reported in 2007 LLR113, the 
Apex Court has clearly observed that an employee who is 
found guilty of committing theft of the property of 
employer deserved dismissal from service. 


14. In the case of AD Sandbor Pune V/s Garware 
Wall Ropes Ltd., reported in 2000 LLR 933, the Hon’ble 
Bombay High Court has held that the powers of the 
Tribunal under Section 11A of the Industrial Disputes Act 
are required to be exercised carefiilly and it is the discretion 
of the Tribtmal to grant or refiise the relief depoiding on 
the facts and circumstances of each case. 

15. In the case of Mahindra & Mahindra Ltd. V/s 
N. B, Narawade2005 Vol. 3 SCC134, theApex Court has 
held that the Tribunal/Labour Court, und^ Section 11A 
of the Industrial Disputes Act is having discretion in 
int^mng with the quantum of punishment awarded by 
the management. However such discretion has been well 
defined by various judgem«its of theApex Court and it 
is certainly not unlimited, The discretion which can be 
exercised under Section llA is available only on the 
existence of c^ain factors like punishment being so 
disproportionate to the gravity of misconduct so as to 
disturb the conscience of the court, or the existence of 
any mitigating circumstances which require the reduction 
of the sentence, or the past conduct of the workman 
which may persuade the Labour Court/Tribunal to reduce 
the punishment. In the absence of any such factor 
existing, the Labour Court/Tribunal cannot by way of 
syn:q)afiiy alone exercise the power imd^ Section 11A of 
the Act and reduce the punishment. ^ 

16. In the case of Regional Manager RSRTC V/s 
Ghanshyam Sharma rqx)rted in 2002 (I) LLJ 234, theApex 
Court has held that there is no place for considering 
misplaced sympathy to reduce punishment and the power 
under Section 11A of the Industrial Disputes Act is to be 
used judiciously. 

17. Many oth^ decisions have been refrared by 
the learned cmmsel for the Party II in their written 
submissions. Howevo', Uie ratio in all these decisions 
is similar wherein it has been consistently held that the 
misconduct if proved regarding the aspect of theft has 
to be considered as grave misconduct irrespective of 
amount involved in it since it is the question of faith of 
the employer on the employee which is a main factor 
and one there is a lost faith, the employer cannot be 
forced to retain such employee in service. Hence, all 
these aspects clearly prove that there is no mitigating 
circunLstance in favour of the employee/workman to be 
considered as the misconduct stands proved which is 
connected with theft and therefore the punishment 
imposed by the management cannot be termed as 
disproportionate. No other circumstances is brought 
on record to invoke the discretion under Section 11A of 
the Industrial Disputes Act in favour of the workman. 
Thus, I answo* the Issue Nos. 3 & 4 in negatve. 

18. Considering the above discusions, it is clear that 
Party I is not entitled for any relief as claimed in the present 
reference and thus the following order.

2. The Second party, Secretary tha-eof has filed 
Statement of claim at Ex. 9. According to th^n, the first 
party is operating in majorports including Ports of Mumbai 
and JNPT and also in various cities in India. More than 
thousand workmen are in the employment of first patty in 
various categories. The first party has issued a general 
notice of retrenchment dt.8-8-2001 to 212 workmen 
informing them Uiat thity were retrenched w. e.f. 10 - 8 - 2001 . 
According to them, second party moved the Regional 
Lal^ur Commissioner ©, Mumbai about the illegal 
termination of 212 workmen and requested to intervene 
advice first party not to act upon the retrenchment 


1842 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA:FEBRUARY 26,2011/PHALGUNA 7,1932 


[Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 


notices. The said dispute was admitted in conciliation. 
Second party had informed to the first party that they had 
not displayed seniority list on notice board. Therefore 
they cannot retrench 212 junior most workers as there is 
no sMUOiity list. 

3. According to second par^, first parly by its letter 
dt. 18-8-2001 informed the union that th^were maintaining 
status quo as on 9-8-2001 accordingly all workers were 
allowed to r^ume their duties. The first party issued 
anothernoticedated 27-8-2001 to 186 workmen out of212 
informing them that th^ would be retrenched fiom services 
w.e.f. 29-8-2001. They informed 26 workers that, thdr 
services would be continued. The second party again 
informed Dy. CLC © about the illegal tramination of 
services of 186 workmen. The conciliation proceedings 
were held on 12-1-2002. However dispute could not be 
settled on account of adamant^ rigid and unlawful attitude 
of first party. The failure report was submitted by 
amciliation officer to Central Govemmait and Govanmait 
has sent the reference to this Tribunal for adjudication. 

4. According to second party, the first party illegally 
terminated the services of 186 workmen under the guise of 
reirenchment notices dated 27-8-2001. The notices are 
illegal and uiyustified. The first party did not comply with 
die provisions of S(x:tion 25F of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947 and Rule 77 ofindustrial Dispute (Central) Rules 
while tominating the services of 186 workmen. The services 
of these workmen were terminated during pendency of 
conciliation proceedings violating provisions of Seaion 
33 of Industrial Disputes Act. The action of first party 
amount to unfair labour practice within the meaning of 
items 13 & 14 of V Schedule of the Industrial Disputes 
Act. Therefore second party prays that the retrraichment 
notices dt. 27-8-2001 be declared illegal and unjustified 
and direction be given to first party to reinstate these 186 
workmen with full backwages and continuity of their 
respective services. 

5. First party resisted the statement of claim vide its 
written statement at Ex. 11. According to it, the Government 
has committed and apparent error of law and fact by 
. sending this reference. It is wrongly mentioned in the 
reference order that, 212 workmen were retrenched. In faa 
only 186 workers were retrenched. The earlier notice to 
212 workmen was already withdrawn. The Tribunal has no 
jurisdiction or power to adjudicate the termination/ 
retrenchment of 186 employees w.e.f. 29-8-2001. Out of 
186 workmen, 11 wo-e working in a supervisory capacity 
and were drawing pay more than Rs. 1,600 p.m. therefore 
those 11 employees are not ‘workmen’ as defm^ under 
Industrial Disputes Act. The saiiority list was displayed 
on the notice board as well as the workplace of the 
employees on 21-8-2001. 

6. According to first party, as per the contract of 
Shipping Corporation of India, it has engaged 232 
employees to carry out the said contract work. It was an 


established practice prevailing in the port that, when 
contractor is changed, the same workers are continued by 
the new contractor/conqiany. As per the order of Hon’ble 
High Court, the first party lost the contract and the new 
contractor, inspite of repeated request did not employ any 
existing en^loyee of the first party. As per the said order 
of Hon’ble High Court, contract was given to M/s. Hillson 
Dinshaw Ltd. w.e.f. 1 -5-2000 to cany out the contract work 
of Shipping Clorporation of India. TTieHon’bleHigh Court 
had also given direction to the new contractor to consider 
these workmen while recruiting new workmen. However 
new contractor neither recruited these woikmen nor 
continued any of them. Therefore first party had no 
alternative but to retrench 186 workmen as it has lost the 
contract work of Shipping Corporation of India. 

7. Alongwith the retrenchment notice, the first party 
sent the cheques of one month’s pay and compensation 
of Hraichment to the respective workmen. TTiey were 
rettEi?ched lawfully as first party was required to close 
down its business at the site of Port Trust of India, Mumbai. 
According to them, the concerned workers are gainfully 
empU yed after their retroichment. In the circiunstances, 
the first party claims that the workers are not entitled for 
any relief as prayed for. Therefore they pray that the 
refifreace be rejected. 

8. Second party filed its rejoinda: at Ex. 12. They 
repeated the same story as they have put in the statement 
of claim and also denied the contents in the wntten 
statement. 

9. In the light of rival pleadings, my Learned 
Predecessors has framed issues at Ex. 13 for determination, 

I record my findings thereon for the reasons to follow: 

Issues 

(i) Whether the action of the employer M/s, Ganesh 
Container Movers Syndicate, Mumbai in 
terminating the services of 212 pamanait workmai 
under the guise of retrenchment is legal and 
justified? 

(a) Whether the employer has closed its business 
from June, 2002? 

(h) What rdief the concerned woikmen are entitled to? 

REASONS 

ISSUE No. 1 

10. In the case at hand, facts are not disputed that 
the first party was the employer and it has served 212 
woikmen with notice of retrenchment. The fact is also not 
disputed that subsequently, the notices were taken back 
and fresh notice of retrenchment were issued to 186 
woikmen whose services wae terminated w.e.f. 29-8-2001. 
The fact is also not disputed that along with the 
retrendiment notice, a cheque of one month’s pay and 
retrenchment compensation was also sent to each 
workman. It is the case of second party union that the 






NT 









('^TFTII—3(ii)] 


26, 2011/'4>MJH 7, 1932 


1843 


action of retrenchment taken by the employer is malatlal. 
Under the guise of retrenchment the employer alleged to 
have illegally terminated the services of 186 woricmen. 

11. It is the case of the first party company that as per 
the order dt. 15-5-2000 passed by Hon’ble Bombay Hi^ 
Court in a WP, the: contract of work of first party was allotted 
to new contractor, Hillson and Dinshaw Ltd., tlierefore first 
party had no alternative but to retrench its workers. In this 
respect, the fact is not disputed evai by the second party 
that as per the order of Hon’ble High Court, the work of first 
party for which these woikmoi were employed was allotted 
to new contractor Hillson and Dinshaw Ltd. According to 
first party as its work was allott^ to new arntraclor, Hillson 
and Dinshaw Ltd., it had no altonative but to retrench tl\e 
workmen employed for the same work. 

12. In this respect, the learned advocate for the 
second party pointed out that the workman not only have 
raised this point, but have also sought direction from the 
Hon’ble High Court to give them preferaice while making 
the appointment of workers by Hillson and Dinshaw Ltd. 
The Learned Advocate pointed out that in notice of motion 
No. 1761/1998 in Suit no. 2308/98, the Hon’ble High Court 
has given directions in clear words that “5th defoidant 
(Hillson and Dinshaw Ltd.) shall be at liberty to engage 
the permanent workers referred to in the bid documait 
submitted to the plaintiffs as also the 26 Drivers and 26 
Cleaners whose numbers are listed in the affidavit of 
defendant no. 5 dated 15-5-2000. However in the event the 
5th defendant requires any additional workers for doing 
the work under the contract the 5th defoidant shall engage 
only workers from amongst the 232 workers whose names 
have been submitted to this Court and copies of which are 
given to the plaintiffs and defendant no. 5. The defendiuit 
no. 5 shall not engage any workers from outside or any 
other workers unless the aforesaid 232 workers are first 
offered eraploymait.” 

13. In the light of the facts and circumstances on 
record, it is clear that the employer was constrained to 
issue the retrenchment notices as their contract of work 
was allotted to new contractor, Hillson and Dinshaw Ltd. 
Therefore, it caimot be said that the retrenchment was at 
the whim of the employer. So also it cannot be said that 
under the guise of retrenchment, the employer has 
terminated the services of the workmen illegally. 

14. In this respect, further I would like to point out 
that the employer has not terminated the services of the 
workmen abruptly. On the other hand, he has issued notices 
to each of the workman. He has also sent alongwith the 
notice, the cheque of one month’s pay and compensation 
for the retrenchment. The witness of union Mr. 
Krishnanandan Pandit in para 3 of his affidavit at Ex. 19 
stated that he had been paid retrenchmait compensation. 
The fact is not disputed even by second party that a 
cheque of one month’s pay and retrenchment 
eompensation was sent to each workman alongwith the 
retrencfimoit notice. It indicates that the employer has 


complied with the requirement of Section 25F of the 
Industrial Wsputes Act. 

15. It is the case of the union tltat there is no seniority 
list publishetl and some workers junior to the retrenched 
186 workers are still in service. However, the second party 
has not pointed out any such instance. Furthermore as 
the first party has lost die contract of work of Shipping 
Corporation of India Ltd. they were compelled to stop 
their activities as the woik contract was given to the new 
contractor Hillson and Dinshaw Ltd. It was allottod to 
new contractor as per the onler of Hon’ble Bombay High 
Court. In the circum.stances, the averment does not stand 
to reasons that the , workers junior to the retrenched 
workers are still working there. In the circumstances, it 
cjinnot be said that first party has terminated the sorice 
of 186 workmtm ill^ally und^ the guise of r^encbmrat. 
On the other hand in the light of facts and circumstances 
discussed therein above, the action of retrenchment 
appeiirs just and proper. Accordingly, I decide this issue 
no. 1 in the affirmative. 

ISSUE No. 2 

16. The retrenchment is held just and proper and 
proper procedure was followed. Furthermore these 
workmen have also approached the Hon’ble High Court. 
Hon’ble Bombay High Court has given direction to the 
new contractor Hillson and Dinshaw Ltd. Not to appoint 
any additional workers at workplace except these 232 
workers whose names have been submitted (who were 
retrenched from the service). It also indicates that the 
retrenchment was neither arbitrary nor at the whim of the 
employer but employer was constrained to take the said 
action as he has lost the work contract, as it was allotted 
to the new contractor. The employer has also paid one 
months’ pay and retrenchmoit compensation to all these 
workers, therefore, I hold that the retrenchment is legal 
and these workmen are not entitled to any rdirf" as claimed 
for. Accordingly, I decide this issue no.2 in the negative. 

ADDITIONAL ISSUE No.1- (a) 

17. It is the averment of the first party that it has 
closed down their business at Mumbai Port since June 
2002. It was denied by second party. However this issue 
has no relevancy with the subject matto' or the point in 
dispute. What retrenchment is held legal and proper, it 
has no relevancy whether the company has closed its 
business since June 2002 or not. In the circumstances, it is 
unnecessary to deal with this issue. Howcvct as this 
additional issue is framed, I am dealing with it. On the 
issue, I would like to point out that witness Shri Jaiprakash 
Khare on behalf of first party dmied in his cross at Ex-34 
that the company is still functioning.Eiji9iermore witness 
of second party Mr. Krishnanand^ Pandit says in his 
cross at Ex-19 last para therein that afier June 2002 after 
closing of the business of Ganesh Containers at Mumbai 
Port Trust he did not ^ply to the High Court about non- 
compliance of order. In short he has ad^tted that in June 
2002, the business of Ganesh Containers was closed down. 



1844 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: FEBRUARY 26,2011/PHALGUNA 7,1932 


[Past II—^ec. 3(ii)] 


There is also no evidence on record to show that business 
of first party is still going on after 2002. In the 
circumstances, I decide this issue no. 1 (a) id the affirmative 
and proceed to pass the following order: 

ORDER 

Action of the Management of 
Ganesh Containers Movers’ 

Syndicate, Mumbai in retrenching 
the workmen is legal and justified.

AWARD 

1. Central Government, MoL, New Dellii vide 
notification No. L-12012/239/99-IR (B-1) dated 9-11-1999, 
has referred the following dispute for adjudication to this 
iribunal- 

2. Whether the action of the management of 
Kshetriya Gramin Bank, Mainpuri, in terminating the 
services of Sri Aditya Prakash DubQ^ and not reinstating 
liim under the provisions 25H is legal and justified ? If 
not to what relief the workman is entitled. 

3. Facts of the case in brief are that the claimant 
was appointed as Assistant cum Cashier by the opposite 
party bank on 11-7-80 and posted at the bank’s BhOgaon 
Branch in District Mainpuri. It is stated that he was 
required to perform all the duties which devolve in this 
bank as Assistant cum Cashier for the full hours of work 
{he branch and the applicant performed his duties to the 
lull satisfaction of his superiors and he was paid salary 
at the prescribed scale of pay on monthly basis. It may 
be pointed out that although there is no mention of the 
dale in the reference order on which claimant allies that 
he was removed from the service by the opposite party. 
However he has given the date of his removal as 13-9-83 
in paragraph no.5 of his claim statement. It is stated that 
lie had worked for more than 240 days in a calendar year 
preceding the date of his termination and as no reasons 
for termination was advised, no notice pay and 
retrenchment compensation was paid to him by the bank 
(lierefore, the bank has breached the p'ovisions of Section 
25 F of the Act. It is stated that the time of termination of 
his services, the applicant was not the junior most tho'eby 
terminating his service is in breach of provisions of 
Section 25G of the Act. It is also stated that the opposite 
party while inducting fresh hands in the service of the 
bank did not provide any, opportunity of re-employment 
to the applicant thus the action of the opposite party bank 
IS in breach of section 25H Of the Act. It is also stated 
ihat after termination of his services he continued 
approaching the Chairman of the bank who always 
assured him to wait and also always assured that He will 
be takai back in s«vice. It is also alleged that finding the 
assurance given by the bank to be follow he raised 


industrial dispute in the year 1990 before the ALC, but 
the apfffopriate government refused to refer the case of 
the 2 q)plicant for adjudication. It is stated diat on coming 
to know of the awards delivered by this Honhle Tribunal, 
the applicant wrote to the government detailing the 
position of refeences of other similar cases whereupon 
the Ministry wrote a letter dated 2-4-98 to the ALC (C) 
Lucknow to consider all such cases. Ultimately the 
reference was made to this tribunal for adjudication by 
the appropriate government. Lastly it is stated that as the 
entire action of the opposite party bank is in gross 
violation of the provisions of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947, therefore, the same is liable to be set aside and the 
workman is entitled to be reinstated in the service of the 
bank with full back wages and all consequmtial benefits. 

4. The claim of die ^licant has been refuted by 
the opposite party on a number of grounds viz, that the 
claimant was engaged as a ten^raiy employee on daily^~. 
wages in Bhogaon Branch of Uie opposite par^ bank; that 
the claimant did not hold any r^ular and permanent post 
in the bank, that the claimant bad never worked for 240 
days in any calendar year or preceding 12 months in the 
bank; that &e services of the claimant had been disengaged 
by the bank with effect from 13 -9-83; that the claimant was 
never interested in his ten^rary nature of duties; he was 
ineg^ular and in sincere in performing his job in the bank; 
that the claimant is trying to seek back door entry by way 
of litigation; that the claimant had no lien or right on any 
regular and permanent post in the bank; that the 
engagement of the claimant was as p^ exigency of work; 
that he did not work continuously against any regular 
vacancy; that the claim of the claimant is highly bdated; 
that the^pointment of the post of clerk on regular basis is 
made through selection board after intoview on the basis 
of suitability, eligibility and other qualifying oiteria; that 
the claim of the claimant is not covered by the provisions 
of Section, 25B, 25F, 25G and 25H of the Act; that the 
claimant is not entitled to any rdi^^mp^ation what so 
ever on the basis of presoit refa'Qice order; that since the 
claimant is not a workman and as such section 2=-A of the 
Act is not (q)plicable in the present ca^ therefore, the 
reference order is luririe to be decided agairistthe claimant 
being bad in law and without jurisdiction. L^tly on the 
basis of above pleadings it has been prayed by fiic opposite 
party that the claim of die workman is basdess, devoid of 
merit and therdiy is liable to be rejected. 

5. Rejoinder has also been filed by the claimant but 
nothing new has been pleaded therein except reiterating 
the facts already plead^ in his statement of claim. 

6. Claimant has filed as many as 7 documoits vide 
list dated 23-11-2001 which is p^erno.9/1. It may bepointed 
out that all the documoits are in the nature of photocopies. 

7. Claimant has also filed affidavit in support of his 
claim. Along with his affidavit the claimant has filed 
photocopy of attendance register. 


540GI/11—17 




1846 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA:FEBRUARY 26,2011/PHALGUNA 7,1932 [Part. H— Sbc. 3(u)] 


8. Whereas the clamant appeared in the witness box 
and examined himsdf as W.W. 1 and against it tiieoj^site 
party examined its witness Sri Ram Kumar Tewari, who is 
Manager, in the bfmk as M. W. 1. 

9. Heard and perused the record. 

10. I have anxiously considered the facts and 
pleading of the case adduced by the parties in the instant 
case in the light of the refermce order. 

11. It may be pointed out that by a bare pansal of 
the tarns of reference order it is quite clear that Ae date of 
termination of the services of the workman has not been 
mentioned in itself. However, even if assuming die fact 
that the whole action of the opposite part bank is violative 
of die {MTOvisions of the Industrial Disputes Act, then a 
normal question arises before the tribunal as to from date 
the claimant should be ordered to be reinstated in the 
service of the bank. It is settled legal position of law that 
the tribunal carmot travel beyond the scope and ambit of 
Section 10(4) of the Industrial Dispute Act, and since th^e 
is no date mentioned in the order of reference showing the 
lamination of the workman, the tribunal ipso facto cannot 
assumed the date of terminatipn of services of the worionan 
as 13-9-83 on the basis of mo-e pleadings of the parties. 

12. Therefore, it is concluded that since there is no 
date of termination of the service of the workman in the 
reference reference order itself, the workman cannot be 
held entided for any relief pursuant to the presait reference 
order. 

13. However it is rnade clear that the reference order 
should be clear and specific and at the same time it was 
also the responsibility of the workman to have mentioned 
the date of termination of his services by the opposite 
party before the Assistant Labour Commissioner while 
endorsing his claim before him. No moition of the date of 
termination of his services in the reference order itself is 
sufficimt to hold that the reference order is bound to be 
answered against the workman for the reasons given in the 
body of the award. 

14. For the r^ons recorded above if is held that 
the claimant is not entitled to any relief in the present 
refer«ice and'the reference is answeredagainst him and in 
favour of the opposite party.

colleagues and the whole of the estjdttishment of Ordnance 
Factory, Chanda is registered under Factory Act and fee 
provisions of the Factory Act are applicable to each mid 
every employee working widiin the premises of fee factory, 
irrespective of their individual activities and as such, they 
are to be paid overtime as per provisions of Factory AiX 
and even employee working at the Head of water works 
and sewerage plapts, which are 8 to 9 kilometers from fee 
factory are paid bvWtime as per provisions of Factories 
Act and the Tribunal of Mumbai Bench in OA No.459/94, 
in the case of Mrs. Manorama John and 68 others Vs U.O.I. 
and others by order dated 20-10-1995 have held that the 
staff working in Ordnance Factory, to whatever categories 
they belong, are governed by Factory Act, The same view 
has been taken by CAT Bench of Madras in OA No.980/89 
and OA No.983/89 decided on 30-9-91 ^d CAT Bench, 
Jabalpur in OA No.363/86 decided on 4-3-1994 has also 
followed the view of CAT, Madras and the relief claimed 
by them is common, which cj^ be granted by one common 
judgement and they have not claimed individual benefit 
and the individual baiefit will flow out of the judgement 
after acceptance of their challenge to the acts of 
discrimination and arbitrmness of the non-applicants. The 
applicants have prayed to direct the non-applicant No.2 
to compute the overtime w^es from 1983 ofeachofthenv 
as per the provisions of Section 59 of the Indian Factories 
Act, to declare that they are oititled to get overtime at 
double the rate as pr the provisions of Factories Act, 1948. 
from 9-12-1985 and to direct the non-applicant to pay the 
arrears of diffo'ence. 

4. The management of the non-applicants refuting 
the allegations made by the applicants, have filed their 
written statement pleading inter-alia that the applicants

Act, 1948 and they are (non-applicants) not causing any

by not following the provisions of Section 59 of the 
Factories Act and the applicants are working in hospital, 
DSC Barracks, Guest House and gardens situated in the 
estate of Ordnance Factory, Chanda, which are not 
connected directly or indirectly or incidentally to any 
production or manufactming process as mentioned in the 


Factories Act and as such, the applicants do not come

the applicants are entitled for production link bonus like

not a yard stick to ^tablish and classify them as worker 
under the Factories Act and Cooks, Vendors, Masalchi, 
cooking in the canteen inside the ordnance factory are* 
paid overtime allowance as the provision of Factories Act, 
as they are directly, indirectly or incidcntidly connected to 
manufacturing process but the applicants are not

situated within the premises of fee Ordnance Factory, 
Estate and as such, not entitled for overtime allowance at 
double the rate and the factory premises, of Ordnance 
Factory, Chanda is different unit than the Cfrdnance Factory 
Estate and the Estate includes diffo-ent establishments 
outside the factory premises like school, hospital, guest 
house, parus etc. for the benefit and wdfare of fee residents
